    window.loadScript = (url, { charset = 'UTF-8', ...attributes } = {}, location = 'head') => {
      const existingScript = document.querySelector('script[src="' + url + '"]');
      if (existingScript) {
        return;
      }

      const script = document.createElement('script');
      script.src = url;
      script.type = 'text/javascript';

      script.async = true;
      script.charset = charset;

      if (attributes) {
        Object.entries(attributes).forEach(([key, value]) => {
          script.setAttribute(key, value);
        });
      }

      if (location === 'head') {
        document.head.appendChild(script);
      } else {
        document.body.appendChild(script);
      }

      return script;
    };

uid="ZTxIJo" component-url="/_astro/GoogleAnalytics.Buaiy_s2.js" component-export="GoogleAnalytics" renderer-url="/_astro/client.CpQ9otmg.js" props="{&quot;title&quot;:[0,&quot;Unmasking the crawls the crawls with Attribution Business Insights&quot;],&quot;slug&quot;:[0,&quot;attribution-business-insights&quot;],&quot;excerpt&quot;:[0,&quot;Cloudflareâs new Attribution Business Insights dashboard helps website owners understand crawler behavior, appetite, and potential value, fueling business-level conversations around crawl compensation.&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;Original content is the lifeblood of conversations and curiosities. Imagine a world without it: we could find a thousand ways to regurgitate the same material thatâs already been created, but we would witness the decline of fresh ideas and arguments.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Website owners fuel the ecosystem of ideas, news, and interesting tidbits, but they face the increasingly complex challenge of managing traffic to their websites and being paid for their content. While some bot traffic is clearly malicious, it isnât always obvious when a particular AI crawler is helping or harming your business. To answer this, site owners need granular, reliable data to differentiate between traffic that provides value, and traffic that strains resources while eroding the foundation of their business model: actual humans consuming their content.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;At Cloudflare, we hold a core belief: website owners have the right to &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/content-independence-day-no-ai-crawl-without-compensation/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;control access to their content&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. We want to help website owners maintain their high-quality content and regulate AI traffic.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To provide much-needed clarity and help website owners take control, weâre excited to announce the new &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/attribution-business-insights/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Attribution Business Insights&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;u&gt; dashboard&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; â designed with business decision-makers and publishers in mind.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;the-new-economics-of-the-internet\&quot;&gt;The new economics of the Internet&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-new-economics-of-the-internet\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;For decades, the business model of the Internet relied on a straightforward, unspoken agreement: website owners allowed search engines to crawl their content and, in return, search engines sent readers back to their pages. This symbiotic relationship, where traditional search engines operated with a balanced &amp;quot;crawl-to-referral&amp;quot; ratio, generated the pageviews needed to sustain advertising, affiliate revenue, and subscriptions. Search index crawlers would scan your content &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/ai-search-crawl-refer-ratio-on-radar/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;a couple of times for each referral sent,&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; so making your website available to crawlers had a clear pipeline to additional revenue. We can think of this as the SEO (Search Engine Optimization) era.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, the explosive rise of AI crawlers and agents has broken this contract, plunging the digital publishing industry into an unprecedented crisis. The Internet is risking a transition into a &amp;quot;zero-click&amp;quot; ecosystem where AI chatbots scrape original content to synthesize instant answers â completely bypassing the original sources. Weâve already seen a marked shift from the SEO-only world into an AEO (Answer Engine Optimization) world, and now conversations around GEO (Generative Engine Optimization) are taking center stage.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The imbalance of this new reality is made clear by the crawl-to-referral ratios we see across the Internet today. While traditional search engines had a more balanced ratio of crawls to legitimate visitors referred, major AI crawlers operate on a drastically different, extractive scale. Bots from leading AI companies have been observed with a range of crawl-to-referral ratios: we noted ratios of 118:1 up to nearly 50,000:1 around the time of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/ai-crawler-traffic-by-purpose-and-industry/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;our Content Independence Day in 2025&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. In other words, an AI crawler might have crawled your premium content tens of thousands of times just to send back a single visitor. This ratio is fundamentally unfair.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For publishers, this creates a double hit: first, theyâre losing out on the crucial referral traffic, ad impressions, and direct audience relationships that fund content creation and journalism. Second, theyâre forced to bear the rising infrastructure costs of hosting and serving content to automated bots that offer no commercial value in return. The era in which it makes sense to allow &lt;b&gt;all&lt;/b&gt; crawlers in the hopes of being discovered is over.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;introducing-attribution-business-insights\&quot;&gt;Introducing Attribution Business Insights&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#introducing-attribution-business-insights\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;We want website owners to have the facts â the cold, hard numbers to understand which bots are helping their business and which bots are harming it. We also want to make this analysis easier than ever, which is why weâve designed Attribution Business Insights to cut the noise, focusing on the details that our customers have told us are most important.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://dash.cloudflare.com/?to=/:account/:zone/analytics/attribution-business-insights\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Attribution Business Insights dashboard&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;b&gt; is available to all Cloudflare Bot Management customers&lt;/b&gt;. The new dashboard is designed to deliver a &lt;i&gt;targeted&lt;/i&gt; view of bot traffic flowing to your website; unlike traditional analytics tools that may require extensive manual filtering, this dashboard provides you with key insights right away.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We set out to answer the most pressing questions for site owners today: &lt;b&gt;How should you think about AI traffic on your websites?&lt;/b&gt; What is the value of different audiences â including humans, non-AI bots, and AI bots? And most importantly, what is your data being used for?Â &lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/44iTYHoY6xZ0tmWlxmVg4k/e4defd336a295f2887ec41bb1ca5a629/image4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1236\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;The new Attribution Business Insights dashboard view, which includes insights about bot traffic overall, a site-wide crawl-to-referral ratio, and the distribution of AI bot traffic vs. organic traffic.Â &lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To answer these questions, the dashboard displays a powerful array of data and insights:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Bot traffic to content pages:&lt;/b&gt; View your overall bot vs. human traffic, as well as the volume of all bots successfully accessing content.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Crawl-to-referral ratios:&lt;/b&gt; See your site-wide crawl-to-referral ratio on the scale of 24 hours, seven days, or 30 days. You can also see crawl-to-referral ratios &lt;i&gt;per bot operator&lt;/i&gt; (per company that owns one or more bots).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Top bots breakdown:&lt;/b&gt; A list of top bots by volume, including their country of origin, bandwidth they take up on your website, and whether youâre currently blocking or allowing them.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Updated classification based on crawler behavior:&lt;/b&gt; We go beyond a generic label of âAI Crawlerâ by classifying crawlers with our updated taxonomy, whether itâs &lt;b&gt;Training&lt;/b&gt; (i.e., training the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/ai/what-is-large-language-model/\&quot; To avoid adding noise to the control plane, Attribution Business Insights is intended to be a hub for &lt;i&gt;thoughtful, filtered analytics&lt;/i&gt;, rather than another place to take action. This dashboard serves as a central source of information, allowing you to investigate before then taking an action in the same rule engine that governs other abuse mitigations. To answer this, site owners need granular, reliable data to differentiate between traffic that provides value, and traffic that strains resources while eroding the foundation of their business model: actual humans consuming their content.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;At Cloudflare, we hold a core belief: website owners have the right to &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/content-independence-day-no-ai-crawl-without-compensation/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;control access to their content&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. This symbiotic relationship, where traditional search engines operated with a balanced &amp;quot;crawl-to-referral&amp;quot; ratio, generated the pageviews needed to sustain advertising, affiliate revenue, and subscriptions. Search index crawlers would scan your content &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/ai-search-crawl-refer-ratio-on-radar/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;a couple of times for each referral sent,&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; so making your website available to crawlers had a clear pipeline to additional revenue. We can think of this as the SEO (Search Engine Optimization) era.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, the explosive rise of AI crawlers and agents has broken this contract, plunging the digital publishing industry into an unprecedented crisis. The Internet is risking a transition into a &amp;quot;zero-click&amp;quot; ecosystem where AI chatbots scrape original content to synthesize instant answers â completely bypassing the original sources. Weâve already seen a marked shift from the SEO-only world into an AEO (Answer Engine Optimization) world, and now conversations around GEO (Generative Engine Optimization) are taking center stage.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The imbalance of this new reality is made clear by the crawl-to-referral ratios we see across the Internet today. While traditional search engines had a more balanced ratio of crawls to legitimate visitors referred, major AI crawlers operate on a drastically different, extractive scale. Bots from leading AI companies have been observed with a range of crawl-to-referral ratios: we noted ratios of 118:1 up to nearly 50,000:1 around the time of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/ai-crawler-traffic-by-purpose-and-industry/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;our Content Independence Day in 2025&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. In other words, an AI crawler might have crawled your premium content tens of thousands of times just to send back a single visitor. This ratio is fundamentally unfair.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For publishers, this creates a double hit: first, theyâre losing out on the crucial referral traffic, ad impressions, and direct audience relationships that fund content creation and journalism. Second, theyâre forced to bear the rising infrastructure costs of hosting and serving content to automated bots that offer no commercial value in return. To avoid adding noise to the control plane, Attribution Business Insights is intended to be a hub for &lt;i&gt;thoughtful, filtered analytics&lt;/i&gt;, rather than another place to take action. This dashboard serves as a central source of information, allowing you to investigate before then taking an action in the same rule engine that governs other abuse mitigations. We also want to be loud and clear about inviting business decision-makers into this dashboard, acknowledging that conversations around AI traffic have a wider set of stakeholders than only security-specialized users.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;whats-next\&quot;&gt;Whatâs next&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#whats-next\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The Attribution Business Insights dashboard is the next critical step in providing website owners with the transparency and control they need to manage evolving AI bot threats, and more broadly, shape the new dynamics of the Internet. declaring Content Independence Day, a dynamic market for monetized content has officially emerged. In this report, we examine how the rise of autonomous AI agents is upending traditional search referrals and detail the new infrastructure required to support a sustainable web economy. &quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;One year ago, we declared &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/content-independence-day-no-ai-crawl-without-compensation/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Content Independence Day&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. At the time, we could see what many in the industry were beginning to sense: the fundamental economics of the Internet were shifting. AI adoption was accelerating, publishers were experiencing rapid declines in referral traffic, and AI companies were crawling the web at unprecedented scale, often without clearly declaring intent, and almost always without compensation.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We changed the defaults. For all new domains on Cloudflare, AI training crawlers would be blocked by default unless domain owners chose otherwise. We didn&amp;#39;t do this to wall off the web. We did it because we believed a healthier ecosystem required transparency, control, scarcity, and ultimately, a market where high-quality content could be valued and exchanged fairly.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;A year later, that market has emerged. But the transformation of the Internet has happened even faster than we anticipated. d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;When looking at the crawlers Cloudflare identifies by purpose, the composition of crawler traffic tells the story clearly:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;52% of crawler requests are now for AI training as of June 2026, up from 22% in Spring 2025.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Mixed-use crawlers (those blending search, agent use, and training) represent over 36% of activity.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Pure search crawling now represents a small and declining share of overall crawler activity, despite remaining critical for publisher visibility. &lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/2as6G3xsTXPJXccFOKsjXx/83a0a06d713f878b1b6ea61563593e0e/f2f4f782-ecdf-48bd-9b88-62fa649d2963.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3368 5\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;918\&quot; height=\&quot;521\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;As AI training becomes a primary driver of crawler activity, the ability to distinguish between discovery and training becomes increasingly important. Mixed-use crawlers blur that distinction, putting content owners in a difficult position: choose between remaining discoverable in the agentic era, and giving away their most valuable content without compensation.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;the-old-business-model-is-gone\&quot;&gt;The old business model is gone&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-old-business-model-is-gone\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;For decades, the economic model of the open web was straightforward. Content creators exchanged access to their content for visibility in search engines, which returned referral traffic. That traffic became the primary mechanism through which publishers, creators, and businesses generated economic value.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;But today, that exchange is breaking down. Content is still being crawled, indexed, and used â but increasingly without corresponding traffic being returned to the source. As AI systems answer questions, compare products, conduct research, and complete tasks directly, information across the open web is increasingly becoming part of AI training and retrieval systems. The existential question this raises is simple: if content is consumed without audiences ever visiting the source, how do content creators sustain themselves?&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/60T7EMNO25yQ4tlA6JHpKl/abc121511a14c89f8c01cca5f70ec65c/BLOG-3368_6.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3368 6\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1600\&quot; height=\&quot;1110\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;the-implications-are-industry-agnostic\&quot;&gt;The implications are industry-agnostic&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-implications-are-industry-agnostic\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The earliest industries to feel the impact were news organizations and media companies. Today, similar dynamics are impacting businesses across retail, software, IT, and finance. Some of the most heavily crawled categories have seen human traffic decline as much as 40% in less than one year.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/5j3sF7FpYa4b5eLGgSXqhs/a1748b3f3feca6b8722a03d6912bcd72/BLOG-3368_7.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3368 7\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;2000\&quot; height=\&quot;1268\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Many publishers are now preparing for what they call &amp;quot;Google Zero&amp;quot; â a world where little to no traffic comes from search referrals.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The implications extend to essentially every industry. Any organization that publishes proprietary information on the Internet will need to understand how to operate in an agentic era. This dynamic matters not just to content owners, but to all of us. The Internet is a critical part of the global economy and one of the world&amp;#39;s most important public resources for surfacing information. Ensuring it remains healthy and sustainable is essential for all.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;part-ii-the-market-has-emerged\&quot;&gt;Part II: The market has emerged&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#part-ii-the-market-has-emerged\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;When we launched Content Independence Day, we committed to three things:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Transparency and control for site owners, enabling them to define how their content is accessed and monetized.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Tools that create scarcity, shifting the balance of power back to content owners.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;A marketplace where content creators and AI companies of all sizes can discover, license, and determine the value of content more efficiently.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;&lt;p&gt;One year later, a market for monetized content is here, and the conditions for a dynamic marketplace are forming.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;transparency-and-control-created-scarcity\&quot;&gt;Transparency and control created scarcity&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#transparency-and-control-created-scarcity\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Historically, publishers have had limited visibility into how AI companies accessed and used their content. As referral traffic declined, that lack of visibility became an economic problem prompting publishers to seek new ways to capture value.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare&amp;#39;s attribution, business intelligence, and enforcement tools gave publishers visibility into AI consumption at the network level â an enforcement mechanism far more effective than voluntary standards like robots.txt. For the first time, publishers could determine how their content was accessed and monetized. That control created scarcity, and drove a supply-and-demand content economy.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;scarcity-created-leverage\&quot;&gt;Scarcity created leverage&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#scarcity-created-leverage\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Publishers that exercised control over access successfully created scarcity, giving them negotiating leverage that led to better deals. For the first time, publishers gained operator-level attribution data â evidence of how often LLMs attempted to access their content, which competitive LLMs were crawling, what their most in-demand URLs were, and what their crawl-to-referral ratios looked like. This reduced information asymmetry in licensing discussions and enabled publishers to negotiate from a position of knowledge.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;leverage-is-changing-the-balance-of-power\&quot;&gt;Leverage is changing the balance of power&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#leverage-is-changing-the-balance-of-power\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;This leverage has empowered our customers. As they have gained greater visibility into how AI systems access and use their content, theyâve become better equipped to understand the implications for their businesses and more confidently articulate the value of the information, brand, and audiences they have built.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;As the balance of power between content owners and AI companies begins to change, a licensing economy is emerging:Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;More than 50 publisher-AI agreements have been signed since 2023.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Major AI companies now actively license content, increasingly recognizing the value of differentiated and premium content.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Collective licensing models continue to emerge and scale.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Large publishers are securing meaningful licensing agreements, demonstrating that content has real economic value within the AI ecosystem.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;The conversation is no longer &lt;i&gt;whether&lt;/i&gt; content should be compensated. The conversation now is &lt;i&gt;how&lt;/i&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;the-market-is-maturing-but-inefficiencies-remain\&quot;&gt;The market is maturing, but inefficiencies remain&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-market-is-maturing-but-inefficiencies-remain\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Early licensing agreements proved demand exists, but licensing today remains largely bespoke and unlikely to fully replace lost referral, advertising, and affiliate revenue. As a result, publishers are increasingly optimizing for AI consumption alongside traditional human discovery while exploring new monetization pathways.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Supply and demand remain difficult to match efficiently, and while thereâs an understanding that not all content carries the same value, content valuation is still unresolved.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;the-google-convergence-problem\&quot;&gt;The Google convergence problem&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-google-convergence-problem\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;No discussion of this market is complete without addressing Google&amp;#39;s unique role. Google remains the dominant gateway to online discovery, accounting for approximately 88% of referral traffic. But increasingly, Google is helping users consume content directly within Google-owned AI experiences.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/4npzNthY9RDNXRhqe0L2z0/f8414329fd1451bfe5e1ce4d4a9cdc97/BLOG-3368_9.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3368 9\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1648\&quot; height=\&quot;940\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Discovery and consumption serve fundamentally different purposes. Search drives users to content, while AI-powered experiences increasingly summarize and reuse it without requiring users to visit the source. Website owners view these activities differently because one generates traffic, while the other increasingly substitutes for it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;These differences become especially important when site owners are deciding who should be allowed to access their content and for what purpose. Most leading AI companies separate discovery crawlers from training crawlers, making it relatively simple for publishers to enable content access for one purpose or the other. Google does not. Today, Google has access to about 2x more information than leading AI companies because Google leverages a mixed-use bot that makes it difficult for customers to participate in Google&amp;#39;s search ecosystem without also participating in Google&amp;#39;s AI ecosystem.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Unlike other AI providers, Googleâs mixed-use crawler also limits transparency for site owners. Because discovery and AI access are combined into a single crawler, publishers cannot tell why Google is accessing their content or distinguish between traffic used for search and traffic used for AI experiences. They also lose the visibility and evidence that comes from being able to allow or block these activities independently at the network level.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This dynamic has accelerated demand for greater transparency and control, as well as new monetization models to better serve both content owners and AI companies of all sizes.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;part-iii-a-unique-view-of-the-ecosystem\&quot;&gt;Part III: A unique view of the ecosystem&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#part-iii-a-unique-view-of-the-ecosystem\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Cloudflare sits at the intersection of the emerging agentic economy.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;More than 20% of the web sits behind Cloudflareâs network. Of the world&amp;#39;s most-visited websites, 36% rely on our network, and more than 40% of the Fortune 500 are Cloudflare customers. Nearly 80% of leading AI companies use Cloudflare, alongside thousands of developers and emerging AI companies.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This unique position gives us visibility into both sides of the market. We see the content owners creating content, the AI companies consuming it, and the signals increasingly connecting them. That perspective has given us a unique view into how the market has evolved over the past year, and what it now requires.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;part-iv-lessons-from-an-emerging-market\&quot;&gt;Part IV: Lessons from an emerging market&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#part-iv-lessons-from-an-emerging-market\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;As publishers and AI companies adapt to a new agentic economy, Cloudflare has gained a clearer understanding of what the ecosystem now needs.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;transparency-must-become-the-standard\&quot;&gt;Transparency must become the standard&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#transparency-must-become-the-standard\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Content owners increasingly need visibility and control over who is accessing their content, how it is being used, and for what purpose. AI companies increasingly recognize that transparency builds trust and reduces friction with publishers. Visibility and enforcement are no longer security concerns alone â they have become business requirements that directly influence licensing negotiations and commercial decision making.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To help make transparency the standard, Cloudflare is continuing to invest in enhanced attribution, measurement, and publisher controls that give content owners greater visibility into and control over how their content is accessed and used.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;As the industry shifts toward greater transparency, we believe that verifiable bot self-identification and declarations of crawl intent are fundamental to a sustainable ecosystem. Today, more than one-third of crawler activity on our network still comes from mixed-use bots that make it impossible for content owners to distinguish crawl intent. We are actively engaging with the ecosystem and investing in tooling to help drive that number to zero by this time next year.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;better-ai-requires-better-signals\&quot;&gt;Better AI requires better signals&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#better-ai-requires-better-signals\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Over the past year, it has become increasingly clear that AI companies need more than access to content. They need better ways to determine what to access, when to access it, and how frequently it has changed. Indiscriminate crawling wastes compute for AI companies and creates unnecessary bandwidth burden for publishers, reducing efficiency across the ecosystem.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We believe better answers require better intelligence. We are investing in real-time freshness signals with richer trust, quality, and relevance to help AI companies discover differentiated information while reducing unnecessary crawling across the web.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;markets-need-better-discovery-before-better-pricing\&quot;&gt;Markets need better discovery before better pricing&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#markets-need-better-discovery-before-better-pricing\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;We believe better discovery must precede better pricing. In order for the market to mature, publishers and AI companies need better information about one another. We are investing in richer market intelligence, content signaling, and capabilities that improve discovery between both sides of the ecosystem, laying the foundation for more scalable market mechanisms over time.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;part-v-building-the-infrastructure-for-the-agentic-internet\&quot;&gt;Part V. Building the infrastructure for the agentic Internet&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#part-v-building-the-infrastructure-for-the-agentic-internet\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;One year ago, Content Independence Day introduced a simple idea: content owners should have greater control over how AI companies access and use their information.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Over the past twelve months, that control helped give rise to a market. Transparency created scarcity. Scarcity created leverage. Leverage accelerated licensing. What was once a theoretical discussion about the future of AI and content has become an active market, with publishers, AI companies, and technology providers all adapting to a new set of economic realities.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The market is now entering a new phase that demands new infrastructure. As the Internet becomes increasingly agentic, the underlying systems that support it must evolve to handle permissions, licensing, and commercial transactions at scale. Content owners and AI companies need more efficient ways to connect and exchange value. We believe these capabilities will converge into programmable, scalable mechanisms for content discovery and monetization â reducing friction while unlocking richer forms of value exchange.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare&amp;#39;s role is to build the infrastructure and business intelligence, and contribute to the standards that allow the market to determine value more efficiently and help publishers and AI companies participate in a healthier, more dynamic content economy.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The Internet has always evolved. This evolution is faster and more consequential than most. But with the right infrastructure, the right incentives, and a commitment to transparency, we believe the agentic Internet can become more sustainable, more efficient, and better for everyone.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Methodology:\n&lt;/b&gt;The data in this report is compiled from &lt;a href=\&quot;https://radar.cloudflare.com/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Radar&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://cloudflare.net/files/doc_downloads/Presentations/2026/06/15/New/Master-Deck-2026-Investor-Day-FINAL.pdf\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Investor Day 2026 Presentation&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare Radar is a hub showcasing global Internet traffic, attack, and technology trends and insights. Day, weâre giving website owners finer options to manage AI traffic. Instead of a one-size-fits-all block, all customers can now easily distinguish and manage Search, Agent, and Training bots, alongside the new ability to protect ad-monetized pages.&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;One year ago, we declared the first &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/content-independence-day-no-ai-crawl-without-compensation/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Content Independence Day&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and we gave website owners the means to take back control of their content. The deal between crawlers and website owners that had held up for 30 years â we crawl you, and you get referrals â was no longer true. AI was taking everything and sending back nothing, presenting an existential threat to website owners. And so we launched a one-click &amp;quot;Block AI Bots&amp;quot; option, along with a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/introducing-pay-per-crawl/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Pay-Per-Crawl marketplace&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;A lot has changed in a year. Last July, conversations around âAI botsâ centered around blocking AI training without compensation, pointing to the winâlose deal where content was used for model training with no value driven back to the website owner. But a desire for more nuance has emerged: Content owners still want to be able to protect their content, and they should be compensated for the original content that they work hard to create, curate, and share. We also know that locking down content isnât a one-size-fits-all solution; website owners want more options than resorting to âblock all automation, every time.â&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If you run a small site, the problem isnât &lt;i&gt;just&lt;/i&gt; that someone could train models on your content â it&amp;#39;s that nobody can find you in the first place. So you have to make a Faustian bargain: either show up in search and let AI train on you, or risk losing discoverability. This unfairly advantages incumbent search providers if they use the same bots for both search and training; and this unfair advantage incentivizes new players to be evasive as they try to close the competitive gap.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;now-ai-can-be-anything\&quot;&gt;Now, AI can be anything&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#now-ai-can-be-anything\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Today, AI can be in anything. Google search has changed from being sorted by AI to being a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.google/products-and-platforms/products/search/search-io-2026/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;full answer engine&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; that answers your question directly on the results page. And Google is not unique in this position â this is the direction in which âsearchâ is moving.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We could debate the cutoff for what qualifies as âAIâ today, just to find that the standard changes tomorrow. So, instead of defining a bot primarily as âAIâ or not, our updated approach to classification will ask deeper questions about bot or agent behavior: What are they doing on my site? What are they storing? And how will they reshare my content?&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;a-pragmatic-taxonomy\&quot;&gt;A pragmatic taxonomy&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#a-pragmatic-taxonomy\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;To address these questions, we need a more nuanced view â a pragmatic taxonomy that aligns with the AI use cases our customers care about. So we are opening the discussion beyond AI training alone and focusing on three AI use cases that we want all customers to be able to manage:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Search:&lt;/b&gt; any behavior that collects or indexes your content, so it can answer questions about it later. The key is that Search is proactively building a database of your site to later respond to queries with. Site owners should expect to get referral traffic or other equitable compensation as a result.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Agent: &lt;/b&gt;automated&lt;b&gt; &lt;/b&gt;behavior that is acting, usually in real time, on a person&amp;#39;s behalf, to get something done right now. This includes chat fetch bots (e.g., ChatGPT-User) and browser-use agents (e.g., Gemini or Claude driving Chrome). The key is that it visits your web application in order to complete a job, and often there&amp;#39;s a human waiting on the other end.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Training&lt;/b&gt;: a crawler taking your content to train or fine-tune a model. The key is that your data is permanently absorbed into the underlying architecture of the AI to improve its capabilities.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Many popular crawlers on the web fall into one of the classifications above; some fall into multiple. We classify plenty of other behaviors beyond the three above â including ads verification, feed fetching, and agentic transactions (more on this below). But we believe it should be simple for all website owners to manage access for these three AI-centered use cases. We believe that bot operators should separate their crawlers because that creates more transparency for website owners: allowing them to better understand why a given crawler is visiting them, as well as to better manage the access they extend to that crawler. If a company runs automation that builds &lt;b&gt;Search&lt;/b&gt; indexes, acts as an &lt;b&gt;Agent&lt;/b&gt;, and collects data to &lt;b&gt;Train&lt;/b&gt; their models, then we strongly encourage that company to separate the automation into three separate crawlers.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We want a classification system that is scalable and representative of the world of automated traffic as it evolves. Tracking a botâs purposes is nothing new, but our new taxonomy involves a few updates that better represent the state of bot traffic today. Most notably, we want to recognize that bots that have multiple purposes should be tracked with all purposes, not just one of them.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;new-options-to-manage-ai-traffic\&quot;&gt;New options to manage AI traffic&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#new-options-to-manage-ai-traffic\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;We want to provide more options for managing different kinds of AI traffic, to &lt;/b&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;i&gt;all&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;b&gt; website owners on the Cloudflare network.&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The managed preset to âBlock AI botsâ that weâve announced in the past included single-purpose bots that crawled data for model training, as shown below:Â &lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/3XlnMWyLXLpLgWLP7hgRGP/d01b9b60c513a7904558fdc674fb74b3/BLOG-3337_2.png\&quot; So, weâre launching the ability to &lt;b&gt;manage AI traffic based on &lt;/b&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;i&gt;three&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;b&gt; major use cases: Search, Agent, and Training&lt;/b&gt; crawlers. With these new options, our customers can more finely tune how they manage AI bot traffic â including customers on our Free tier.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/4ffejfK0AQNX7vPro0cwhK/7cc9eafa05975001fa2f614a725aeb7f/BLOG-3337_3.png\&quot; &lt;b&gt;for each of these three classifications.&lt;/b&gt; For all new domains onboarding to Cloudflare, the categories of &lt;b&gt;Training&lt;/b&gt; and &lt;b&gt;Agent&lt;/b&gt; will be blocked by default &lt;b&gt;on the pages that display ads, &lt;/b&gt;while &lt;b&gt;Search&lt;/b&gt; will remain allowed by default.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;An ad is a signal that a website owner meant for a person to land there and see it â something monetizable that fuels the business. So, on those pages, we treat human attention as the end goal, and keep away the bots that may prevent this attention (i.e., Training and Agent bots). On the other hand, Search is the behavior that most naturally funnels back visitors, and we believe itâs in the interest of most site owners to allow this.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Another change that will apply on September 15 is that multi-purpose crawlers (specifically those that combine Search with Training) will be allowed/blocked according to &lt;i&gt;all&lt;/i&gt; of their behaviors, in line with our call for transparency for website owners. Since the defaults will be enforced by the most restrictive applicable rules, multi-purpose crawlers such as Googlebot, Applebot, and BingBot will be blocked by customers who have selected to block Training (either through the new options to &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/additional-configurations/block-ai-bots/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;manage AI traffic&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, or through the legacy Block AI bots service).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Of course, customer choice is paramount: if a website owner wants to opt out of these new default configurations, they can &lt;a href=\&quot;https://dash.cloudflare.com/?to=/:account/:zone/security/settings\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;easily mark this in their Security settings&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; any time leading up to September 15, which will confirm that they want &lt;i&gt;no changes&lt;/i&gt; on Training crawlers that also crawl for Search purposes. Weâll also continue to notify customers of the upcoming change to defaults as we approach September 15 to ensure that customers who want to choose settings different from the defaults have the opportunity to do so.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;botbase-a-new-visibility-plane-for-enterprise-customers\&quot;&gt;BotBase: a new visibility plane for Enterprise customers&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#botbase-a-new-visibility-plane-for-enterprise-customers\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Weâre also excited to launch a major visibility update as a new feature of Enterprise Bot Management. As Cloudflareâs directory of tracked bots has grown, so has the desire to manage these bots in sensible groupings and to understand more detail about a particular bot.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Introducing &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/botbase/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;BotBase&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. BotBase is our new database tracking all known bots, including Verified bots and agents. This database provides a comprehensive, searchable view of our entire directory of bots, directly on the Cloudflare dashboard. Weâre tackling &lt;i&gt;visibility first&lt;/i&gt;, but, later this year, weâll expand BotBase to provide a direct control center for known automated content on your website.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;With this new view, Enterprise Bot Management customers can see the full catalogue of all Verified bots/agents and where they are classified in this updated taxonomy â a view weâve never shown dynamically on the Cloudflare dashboard before. Customers who want to precisely target a specific bot can also easily filter for all traffic from this bot, plus copy the detection ID to use in Security rules. All of this is now live within a dedicated page, which can be accessed through the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://dash.cloudflare.com/?to=/:account/:zone/security/settings/bot-traffic/bot-base\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Bot Management configuration card&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;As we built BotBase, we wanted to account for all of the pieces of information that would allow us to build scalable, powerful insights from bot to bot. One of these pieces is a cornerstone for our updated taxonomy, which is &lt;b&gt;based on what a bot may do on your site â its behavior.&lt;/b&gt; We separate these classifications as shared below, use.â This setting can be set to one of three levels, from least to most permissive:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;immediate&lt;/code&gt; â interact, but store and reuse decisions in sensible groupings rather than manage individual bot-by-bot rules&lt;b&gt;.&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To further support this, starting today, we&amp;#39;re testing a new signal, &lt;code&gt;use&lt;/code&gt;, that extends &lt;a href=\&quot;https://contentsignals.org/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Content Signals&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and lives in your robots.txt. This extends the three fields of the first version of Content Signals with a fourth, optional field that expresses the same preference as above:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;use=immediate&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;use=reference&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;use=full&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;As with all other items listed in the robots.txt file, the values of content use signal a website ownerâs &lt;i&gt;preference&lt;/i&gt;, rather than issuing blocks directly. Weâre now adding support for this extension: all customers who have already enabled managed robots.txt â which prepends the preference to robots.txt that crawling for search is okay, but that crawling for training is not â will now have the additional preference of &lt;code&gt;use=reference&lt;/code&gt; added to their robots.txt.&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;# Cloudflare Managed content with original Content Signals\n\nUser-agent: *\nContent-Signal: search=yes,ai-train=no\nAllow: /&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;The contents of Cloudflare managed robots.txt with the original Content Signals values. &lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-Typescript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-Typescript\&quot;&gt;# Cloudflare Managed content with the new content-use signal\n\nUser-agent: *\nContent-Signal: search=yes,ai-train=no,use=reference\nAllow: /&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;The contents of Cloudflare managed robots.txt with the added parameter.&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Weâre also starting to track content uses for every bot in BotBase, and when we discover a bot abusing these signals, it will lose the âVerifiedâ status, resulting in it no longer being allowed. Today, bots that reproduce in full cannot have the Verified status.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;what-does-it-mean-for-a-bot-to-be-verified\&quot;&gt;What does it mean for a bot to be Verified?&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-does-it-mean-for-a-bot-to-be-verified\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Speaking of âVerified,â the definition of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/concepts/bot/verified-bots/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Verified&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; is being updated to reflect the upcoming changes to default allow and block baselines. Previously, &lt;i&gt;all&lt;/i&gt; Verified bots were allowed by default, which was reflected in our basic &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/get-started/bot-fight-mode/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Bot Fight Mode&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; offering to block unwanted automatic traffic and in our rule templates for Enterprise Bot Management customers.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Starting today, weâre adjusting this to add nuance: non-verified bots are still default blocked, but we are no longer viewing Verified as âdefault allowed.â Now, the Verified label makes a bot allowable with its relevant category, meaning the &lt;i&gt;allowed category&lt;/i&gt; (e.g., allowing Search) will determine what is allowed to access a website.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To balance this change, weâre opening up the process of becoming a Verified bot, and making it more transparent, too. To &amp;quot;Verify&amp;quot; a bot, a bot operator needs to show two things: that you represent yourself honestly, &lt;i&gt;and&lt;/i&gt; you don&amp;#39;t abuse the access that honesty earns. And to make this easier on bot operators, weâre currently building management tools for bot operators to better ensure they are accurately represented by Cloudflareâs classification system (to be announced in the near future).Â &lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/4QMZhQvcXGxpLnazN2qwgW/d00f7e8176e8471fa94725379737259b/BLOG-3337_4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3337 4\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1080\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;A preview screenshot of the upcoming platform built directly for bot operators who are part of or want to be a part of BotBase, the next generation of the Cloudflare Bots Directory.&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;experimenting-with-transitive-trust\&quot;&gt;Experimenting with transitive trust&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#experimenting-with-transitive-trust\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;One more piece: The bot (or agent) at your door increasingly isn&amp;#39;t run by the company that built it. A platform like Cloudflareâs Developer Platform runs automations for thousands of different operators at once, ranging from enterprises to a developer you&amp;#39;ve never heard of. You might trust Stripe, but you don&amp;#39;t necessarily trust everyone who wired Stripe&amp;#39;s tools into a weekend project.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We call the case of (site owner â bot owning company â end user) a matter of &lt;b&gt;transitive trust&lt;/b&gt;, and we&amp;#39;re proposing to utilize the existing Forwarded header as defined in &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.rfc-editor.org/info/rfc7239\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;RFC 7239&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; that rides along with the request and allows âproxy components to disclose information lost in the proxying process.âÂ &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This is similar to what &lt;code&gt;X-Forwarded-For&lt;/code&gt; does for IP addresses, or &lt;code&gt;X-Forwarded-Host&lt;/code&gt; does to preserve the original Host header. So when a website owner says, &amp;quot;Allow this operator,&amp;quot; that preference will hold, whether the operator comes to you directly or through three layers of intermediaries that are trusted. More details can be found in &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/reference/bot-verification/web-bot-auth/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;our documentation&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, with a brief example to show the format below.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;Forwarded: for=&amp;quot;openai&amp;quot;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Adding the extension with content-use discussed above, the header addition would look something like the below, specifying how the operator says they will use the content they access:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;Forwarded: for=&amp;quot;openai&amp;quot;;use=&amp;quot;reference&amp;quot;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This also lines up the incentive model we want to foster. Losing trusted status across the more than 20% of web domains that sit behind Cloudflare is a deterrent with teeth. Trust becomes something you can carry with you, and something you can lose.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;However, as &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/past-bots-and-humans/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;bot traffic blends with human traffic&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, itâs possible that this system of transitive trust doesnât carry beyond the users who can afford to be identifiable. The measures we are proposing today help to convey trust, but they wonât fit the entire web for all time. Small sources of traffic &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/internet-privacy/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;need privacy&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and companies that want to preserve their own privacy commitments should be able to explore fair building blocks for the future of an agentic Internet, such as &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/private-rate-limiting/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;private rate limiting&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;set-your-terms-today\&quot;&gt;Set your terms today&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#set-your-terms-today\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;These are small changes that move in the same direction: site owners get more control over who uses their content, and how. We believe the new defaults we discussed today and will soon implement are ones that encourage transparency and are more reflective of where the world is going.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Of course, the ebbs and flows of the web will continue shifting under us, and we&amp;#39;ll keep adjusting with it. But the direction won&amp;#39;t change, because it&amp;#39;s the one Cloudflare started with: a web ecosystem built around trust. Where the people who make things can decide how they&amp;#39;re used â and one where being honest about what you do earns you more access, not less.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;These new options to manage AI traffic are live now, and can be configured by all existing customers in their &lt;a href=\&quot;https://dash.cloudflare.com/?to=/:account/:zone/security/settings\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;zone Settings&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.
